# Proposed OEIS comments: A234485 to A236273

One section per OEIS entry: the date the result was obtained, the paper that proves it, what the entry still records as unsettled, and the text proposed for posting on that entry.

**Nothing here has been posted.** See [../SUBMITTING.md](../SUBMITTING.md).

---

## A234485

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234485>  
Result obtained: **31 August 2026**  
Paper: [7419](../papers/07001-07500/07419-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 13*a(n-1) -43*a(n-2) -107*a(n-3) +865*a(n-4) -1033*a(n-5) -1916*a(n-6) +2822*a(n-7) +2712*a(n-8) -1332*a(n-9) -1512*a(n-10) -288*a(n-11).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 256 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 10. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A234490

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234490>  
Result obtained: **1 September 2026**  
Paper: [2881](../papers/02501-03000/02881-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical for column k:

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrences for columns k = 1 and 2 are true. Each column counts arrays of a fixed width, and counting those a row at a time gives a transfer matrix, so every column satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most the number of states; checking the ones above is then a finite exact computation, and they hold. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A234545

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A234545>  
Result obtained: **2 September 2026**  
Paper: [281](../papers/00001-00500/00281-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 49 (see link above).

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 1296 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 49, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. ```
```

> Empirical g.f.: 4*x*(39 - 150*x - 278*x^2 + 848*x^3 + 768*x^4) / (1 - 14*x + 31*x^2 + 94*x^3 - 192*x^4 - 192*x^5). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 19 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition on a subblock depends only on which values the block holds and how many times, not on where in the block they sit, so it is decided by the few consecutive lines the block spans. Carrying those lines as the state makes the admissible arrays the walks of a finite digraph with 6 vertices, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 4. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A235845

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235845>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [4201](../papers/04001-04500/04201-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical recurrence of order 55 (see link above)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The recurrence in the linked file is correct, and it is the one below. Counting the arrays a row at a time gives a transfer matrix with 72 states, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that, and Berlekamp-Massey on exact terms returns the minimal one. Its order is 55, the order stated here, and so is the order of every tail. Any recurrence of that order the sequence satisfies therefore has the same characteristic polynomial, so it is this one. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A235974

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A235974>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9233](../papers/09001-09500/09233-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 4*a(n-1) + 7*a(n-2) - 22*a(n-3) + 8*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: x*(39 + 25*x - 182*x^2 + 72*x^3) / ((1 - 5*x + 2*x^2)*(1 + x - 4*x^2)). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 20 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition on a subblock depends only on which values the block holds and how many times, not on where in the block they sit, so it is decided by the few consecutive lines the block spans. Carrying those lines as the state makes the admissible arrays the walks of a finite digraph with 4 vertices, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

## A236271

OEIS entry: <https://oeis.org/A236271>  
Result obtained: **7 September 2026**  
Paper: [9234](../papers/09001-09500/09234-PROOF.pdf)

What the entry records as unsettled:

> Empirical: a(n) = 5*a(n-1) + 4*a(n-2) - 16*a(n-3) - 8*a(n-4).
> Empirical g.f.: 2*x*(21 - x - 80*x^2 - 36*x^3) / ((1 - 2*x)*(1 - 3*x - 10*x^2 - 4*x^3)). - _Colin Barker_, Oct 21 2018

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition on a subblock depends only on which values the block holds and how many times, not on where in the block they sit, so it is decided by the few consecutive lines the block spans. Carrying those lines as the state makes the admissible arrays the walks of a finite digraph with 4 vertices, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 3. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```

> Empirical: a(n) = 23*a(n-1) -16*a(n-2) -2232*a(n-3) +7199*a(n-4) +81562*a(n-5) -305840*a(n-6) -1579897*a(n-7) +5702786*a(n-8) +18527773*a(n-9) -56010227*a(n-10) -138644483*a(n-11) +297115491*a(n-12) +656260628*a(n-13) -774956030*a(n-14) -1816063888*a(n-15) +616468708*a(n-16) +2397532500*a(n-17) +617130944*a(n-18) -763319696*a(n-19) -368062992*a(n-20) +11133168*a(n-21) +22287808*a(n-22) +1286144*a(n-23) -327168*a(n-24) -26624*a(n-25)

Proposed OEIS comment:

```
The empirical recurrence is true. The condition on a subblock depends only on which values the block holds and how many times, not on where in the block they sit, so it is decided by the few consecutive lines the block spans. Carrying those lines as the state makes the admissible arrays the walks of a finite digraph with 25 vertices, so a(n) satisfies a constant-coefficient linear recurrence of order at most that; checking the one above is then a finite exact computation, and it holds for n > 24. - _Adrian Perez Fontelles_, Sep 06 2026
```
